It's really just been those intense shifts in my mood and energy.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about how long these episodes of intense happiness or irritability typically last, and how they affect your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: These episodes of high energy and mood swings have been going on for about six months now. During these times, I feel an overwhelming surge of energy and get easily irritated over minor things, which makes it really hard to settle down. It has made interacting calmly with the people around me very difficult because I snap so easily.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to focus or pay attention to details, or do you often find yourself getting distracted, even when you're not experiencing these episodes of high energy or mood swings?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that. I haven't noticed having any specific problems with focusing or getting distracted when my mood is stable.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any situations, even small ones, where you acted on impulse without fully thinking through the consequences, such as interrupting others, blurting out answers, or having difficulty waiting for your turn?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those kinds of impulsive behaviors. I haven't noticed myself interrupting others, blurting out answers, or having trouble waiting my turn.\n\nDoctor: You've mentioned feeling extremely happy and having a lot of energy, but can you confirm if these episodes of high energy and mood elevation have ever caused significant problems in your social or occupational life, or if they've been noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't thought much about how it has affected my work, and I'm not certain if other people have noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to recall any specific instances where your mood swings or high energy levels may have affected your relationships with family, friends, or colleagues, or perhaps impacted your performance at work or school?\n\nPatient: When my energy is really high, I tend to start too many tasks and projects at once, which makes it hard to get anything done. On top of that, my rapid mood swings and tendency to get easily irritated or aggressive over minor things have caused a lot of friction and strained my relationships with my family and friends.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of abnormally elevated or irritable mood that lasted for at least 7 days, or less if hospitalization was required, and was accompanied by at least three symptoms such as inflated self-esteem, decreased need for sleep, more talkative than usual, flight of ideas, distractibility, increase in goal-directed activity, or excessive involvement in pleasurable activities that have a high potential for painful consequences?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've definitely experienced those periods of elevated and highly irritable moods over the last six months, lasting much longer than a week. During these times, I have overflowing energy, start multiple projects at once, and feel a constant, restless need to keep moving. I'm not sure about the other symptoms you mentioned, but my mood definitely shifts rapidly between euphoria and irritability.\n\nDoctor: Did these episodes of abnormally elevated or irritable mood require hospitalization, or did they cause significant impairment in social or occupational functioning, such as problems at work, school, or with relationships, to the point where you were unable to function?\n\nPatient: No, I have never been hospitalized for these episodes. I'm also not really sure how to define the level of impairment it causes in my daily life, so I can't say for certain if it reaches that point."
  }
]

During these times, I have overflowing energy, start multiple projects at once, and feel a constant, restless need to keep moving.
